V_{ij}=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{1}\int_{-\infty}^{1}(\int^{\infty}_{-g_{N,n}(x)}[\mathcal{A}_{N,n}(-z)]_{i}dz)(\int^{\infty}_{-g_{N,m}(y)}[\mathcal{A}_{N,m}(-z)]_{j}dz)\times

(6.128)

\frac{d}{dx}(\frac{h_{N,i}(x)}{g_{N,n}^{\prime}(x)})\frac{d}{dy}(\frac{l_{N,j}(y)}{g_{N,m}^{\prime}(y)})\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)dxdy.

We now collect the following asymptotics for
x\in(1,\infty)
(see
[
1
]
)

(6.129)

\sqrt{\pi}\mathrm{Ai}(-x)=\frac{1}{x^{1/4}}\sin(\frac{2}{3}x^{3/2}+\pi/4)+O(x^{-7/4}),\;\;\;\;\sqrt{\pi}\mathrm{Ai}^{\prime}(-x)=-x^{1/4}\cos(\frac{2}{3}x^{3/2}+\pi/4)+O(x^{-5/4}),

(6.130)

\sqrt{\pi}\int^{\infty}_{x}\mathrm{Ai}(-y)dy=-\frac{1}{x^{3/4}}\sin(\frac{2}{3}x^{3/2}-\frac{\pi}{4})+O(x^{-9/4}),

(6.131)

\sqrt{\pi}\int_{x}^{\infty}\mathrm{Ai}^{\prime}(-y)dy=-\frac{1}{x^{1/4}}\cos(\frac{2}{3}x^{3/2}-\frac{\pi}{4})+O(x^{-7/4}).

For now we only take from these that these functions are bounded, so that for fixed
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and
x\in[1-\delta,1]

(6.132)

[\mathcal{A}_{N,k}(x)]_{l}=O(N^{-(-1)^{l}/6}),\;\;\;\;\int_{-g_{N,k}(x)}^{\infty}[\mathcal{A}_{N,k}(-z)]_{l}dz=O(N^{-(-1)^{l}/6}).

Employing these bounds, and recalling that
g_{N,k}(x)=O(N^{2/3})
, we see that for any
i,j=1,2
the integrals
III_{ij},

IV_{ij}
and
V_{ij}
have order
O(N^{-4/3-(-1)^{i}/6-(-1)^{j}/6})
. Further employing (
6.103
) we see that

(6.133)

\int_{-\infty}^{1}[\mathcal{A}_{N,n}(g_{N,n}(x))]_{i}h_{N,i}(x)dx=O(N^{-(-1)^{i}/6-2/3}).

Thus we see as well that
II_{ij}
has order
O(N^{-4/3-(-1)^{i}/6-(-1)^{j}/6})
. In particular, if
(i,j)\neq(1,1)
all integrals except for
I_{ij}
are
O(N^{-4/3})
.

If
i=j=1
though, none of these bounds are sufficient. Instead we will need to note that for
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and
x\in(1-\delta,1]

(6.134)

[\mathcal{A}_{N,k}(x)]_{1}=O((1-x)^{-1/4}),\;\;\;\;\int_{-g_{N,k}(x)}^{\infty}[\mathcal{A}_{N,k}(-z)]_{1}dz=O(N^{-1/3}(1-x)^{-3/4}).

Employing (
6.132
) for
x\in(1-N^{-2/3},1]
and (
6.134
) elsewhere we see that there is
C
such that

(6.135)

|III_{11}|\leq\frac{C}{N^{4/3}}\left(\frac{1}{N^{2/3}}\int_{N^{-2/3}}^{\infty}\frac{dx}{x^{6/4}}+N^{1/3}\int_{0}^{N^{-2/3}}dx\right)=\frac{3C}{N^{5/3}}.

Similarly we see that
IV_{11},V_{11}
are
O(N^{-4/3})
.

Lastly, by applying (
6.103
) to
II_{11}
, we see that

(6.136)

II_{11}=-\frac{N^{1/3}}{2\pi}\frac{h_{N,1}(1)}{g_{N,n}^{\prime}(1)}\frac{l_{N,1}(1)}{g_{N,m}^{\prime}(1)}\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)^{2}+O(N^{-4/3})=-\frac{\phi(1)\varphi(1)}{18\pi N}[T_{1}(1)]_{11}^{2}+O(N^{-4/3}).

Now we are left to consider
I_{ij}
. As before, the case of
i=j=1
will present an additional subtlety. Let us denote

(6.137)

\mathcal{S}_{N}(x)=\begin{bmatrix}|f_{N,n}(x)|^{-1/4}\sin((N-n)s_{N,n}(x)+\pi/4)\\
-|f_{N,n}(x)|^{1/4}\cos((N-n)s_{N,n}(x)+\pi/4)\end{bmatrix},

(6.138)

\mathcal{C}_{N}(x)=\begin{bmatrix}-|f_{N,m}(x)|^{-1/4}\sin((N-m)s_{N,m}(x)-\pi/4)\\
-|f_{N,m}(x)|^{1/4}\cos((N-m)s_{N,m}(x)-\pi/4)\end{bmatrix}.

We observe that by applying the asymptotics of (
6.131
), for
1-\delta\leq x\leq 1-N^{-2/3}
and
i=1,2

(6.139)

[\mathcal{A}_{N,n}(g_{N,n}(x))]_{i}=[\mathcal{A}_{N,n}((N-n)^{2/3}f_{N,n,1}(x))]_{i}=\pi^{-1/2}[\mathcal{S}_{N}(x)]_{i}+O(N^{-1}(1-x)^{-7/4}),

(6.140)

\int_{-g_{N,m}(x)}^{\infty}[\mathcal{A}_{N,m}(-z)]_{i}dz=(N-m)^{1/2}|f_{N,m,1}(x)|^{1/2}\int_{-(N-m)^{2/3}f_{N,m,1}(x)}^{\infty}[\mathcal{A}_{N,m}(-z)]_{i}dz=

(6.141)

\pi^{-1/2}(N-m)^{-1/3}|f_{N,m,1}(x)|^{-1/2}[\mathcal{C}_{N}(x)]_{i}+O(N^{-4/3}(1-x)^{-9/4}).

The subtlety that arises is that direct substitution of these asymptotic into
I_{11}
, even neglecting the error terms, leads to a quantity that is not integrable. To deal with this, we will need to treat the integral of
I_{ij}
over the regions
[1-N^{-1/3},1]
and
[1-\delta,1-N^{-1/3}]
separately. In the latter integral, we will be able to apply (
6.141
), while the integral over the prior will be negligible unless
i=j=1
, where it will surprisingly cancel the contribution from
II_{11}
up to lower order terms.

To begin, we note that by (
6.141
) for
i,j=1,2
and
1-\delta\leq x\leq 1-N^{-2/3}

(6.142)

[\mathcal{A}_{N,n}(g_{N,n}(x))]_{i}\int^{\infty}_{-g_{N,m}(x)}[\mathcal{A}_{N,m}(-z)]_{j}dz-\pi^{-1}N^{-1/3}|f_{N,m,1}(x)|^{-1/2}[\mathcal{S}_{N}(x)]_{i}[\mathcal{C}_{N}(x)]_{j}=

(6.143)

O(N^{-7/3}(1-x)^{-4}).

We
now note that

(6.144)

N^{-7/3}\int_{N^{-1/3}}^{\infty}x^{-4}dx=O(N^{-4/3}).

Employing this and (
6.143
) we see that

(6.145)

\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{1-N^{-1/3}}[\mathcal{A}_{N,n}(g_{N,n}(x))]_{i}(\int^{\infty}_{-g_{N,m}(x)}[\mathcal{A}_{N,m}(-z)]_{j}dz)\frac{h_{N,i}(x)l_{N,j}(x)}{g_{N,m}^{\prime}(x)}dx=

(6.146)

\frac{1}{\pi^{2}(N-m)}\int_{-\infty}^{1-N^{-1/3}}[\mathcal{S}_{N}(x)]_{i}[\mathcal{C}_{N}(x)]_{j}\frac{h_{N,i}(x)l_{N,j}(x)}{f_{N,m,1}(x)^{1/2}f_{N,m,1}^{\prime}(x)}dx+O(N^{-4/3}).

Now if
(i,j)=(1,2)
or
(i,j)=(2,1)
then for
1-\delta\leq x\leq 1-N^{-2/3}

(6.147)

[\mathcal{A}_{N,n}(g_{N,n}(x))]_{i}\int^{\infty}_{-g_{N,m}(x)}[\mathcal{A}_{N,m}(-z)]_{j}dz=O(N^{-1/3}x^{-1/2}),

and
O(1)
for
1-N^{-2/3}\leq x\leq 1
. Combining both of these, we see that

(6.148)

\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{1-N^{-1/3}}^{1}[\mathcal{A}_{N,n}(g_{N,n}(x))]_{i}(\int^{\infty}_{-g_{N,m}(x)}[\mathcal{A}_{N,m}(-z)]_{j}dz)\frac{h_{N,i}(x)l_{N,j}(x)}{g_{N,m}^{\prime}(x)}dx=O(N^{-4/3}).

The case of
(i,j)=(2,2)
follows more simply by just employing (
6.132
) to obtain (
6.148
). Now we will focus on the case of
(i,j)=(1,1)
. We note that by Taylor’s Theorem
f_{N,n,1}(x)-f_{N,m,1}(x)=O(N^{-1})
uniformly for
x\in(1-\delta,1+\delta)
. Moreover we note that by the above asymptotic for
\mathrm{Ai}^{\prime}
there is
C
such that for
x\in(-\infty,0)
we have that
|\mathrm{Ai}^{\prime}(x)|\leq C(1+|x|^{1/4})
. Thus by again applying Taylor’s Theorem we see that for
1-N^{-1/3}\leq x\leq 1
,

(6.149)

|[\mathcal{A}_{N,n}(g_{N,n}(x))]_{1}-[\mathcal{A}_{N,m}(g_{N,m}(x))]_{1}|=O(N^{-1/6}+(1-x)^{1/4}).

From this, we see that there is
C
such that

(6.150)

|\int_{1-N^{-1/3}}^{1}([\mathcal{A}_{N,n}(g_{N,n}(x))]_{1}-[\mathcal{A}_{N,m}(g_{N,m}(x))]_{1})(\int^{\infty}_{-g_{N,m}(x)}[\mathcal{A}_{N,m}(-z)]_{1}dz)\frac{h_{N,1}(x)l_{N,1}(x)}{g_{N,m}^{\prime}(x)}dx|\leq

(6.151)

CN^{-1}\int_{1-N^{-1/3}}^{1}\frac{N^{-1/6}+(1-x)^{1`/4}}{(1-x)^{3/4}}dx=O(N^{-7/6}).

In addition, we note that by integration by parts we may write

(6.152)

\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{1-N^{-1/3}}^{1}[\mathcal{A}_{N,m}(g_{N,m}(x))]_{1}(\int^{\infty}_{-g_{N,m}(x)}[\mathcal{A}_{N,m}(-z)]_{1}dz)\frac{h_{N,1}(x)l_{N,1}(x)}{g_{N,m}^{\prime}(x)}dx=I^{\prime}+II^{\prime},

(6.153)

I^{\prime}=\frac{1}{2\pi}\bigg[\frac{h_{N,1}(x)l_{N,1}(x)}{g_{N,m}^{\prime}(x)g_{N,m}^{\prime}(x)}\bigg(\int^{\infty}_{-g_{N,m}(x)}[\mathcal{A}_{N,m}(-z)]_{1}dz\bigg)^{2}\bigg]_{1-N^{-1/3}}^{1},

(6.154)

II^{\prime}=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{1-N^{-1/3}}^{1}\frac{d}{dx}(\frac{h_{N,1}(x)l_{N,1}(x)}{g_{N,m}^{\prime}(x)g_{N,m}^{\prime}(x)})\bigg(\int^{\infty}_{-g_{N,m}(x)}[\mathcal{A}_{N,m}(-z)]_{1}dz\bigg)^{2}.

Using (
6.132
) we see that
II^{\prime}=O(N^{-4/3})
and using (
6.134
) that

(6.155)

I^{\prime}=\frac{\phi(1)\varphi(1)}{18\pi N}[T_{1}(1)]_{11}^{2}+O(N^{-4/3}).

We observe that this is the negation of asymptotic for
II_{11}
obtained above. In particular, combining all of these results, we see that the left-hand side of (
6.118
) may be rewritten as

(6.156)

\frac{1}{\pi^{2}(N-m)}\sum_{i,j=1,2}\int_{-\infty}^{1-N^{-1/3}}[\mathcal{S}_{N}(x)]_{i}[\mathcal{C}_{N}(x)]_{j}\frac{h_{N,i}(x)l_{N,j}(x)}{f_{N,m,1}(x)^{1/2}f_{N,m,1}^{\prime}(x)}dx+O(N^{-7/6}).

Combining the relation
s^{\prime}_{N,m}(x)=f_{N,m,1}(x)^{1/2}f_{N,m,1}^{\prime}(x)
, Lemma
6.8
, and the bounds on
R_{N},\bar{R}_{N}
, as in the proof of Lemma
6.6
, we see that the integral of (
6.156
) may further be rewritten as

(6.157)

\frac{1}{\pi(N-m)}\int_{-\infty}^{1-N^{-1/3}}[T_{\infty}(x)\begin{bmatrix}e^{i(N-n)s_{N,n}(x)}\\
e^{-i(N-n)s_{N,n}(x)}\end{bmatrix}]_{1}[T_{\infty}(x)e^{-i\frac{\pi}{2}\sigma}\begin{bmatrix}e^{i(N-m)s_{N,m}(x)}\\
e^{-i(N-m)s_{N,m}(x)}\end{bmatrix}]_{1}\frac{\phi(x)\varphi(x)}{s_{N,m}^{\prime}(x)}dx+O(N^{-7/6})=

(6.158)

\frac{-i}{\pi(N-m)}\sum_{k,l=1,2}\int_{-\infty}^{1-N^{-1/3}}[T_{\infty}(x)]_{1k}[T_{\infty}(x)]_{1l}e^{-(-1)^{k}i(N-n)s_{N,n}(x)-(-1)^{l}i(N-m)s_{N,m}(x)}(-1)^{l}\frac{\phi(x)\varphi(x)}{s_{N,m}^{\prime}(x)}dx

We will show that the terms in this equation when
k=l
are negligible. For this, we observe that by integration by parts, we may write the
(k,l)=(1,1)
term in (
6.158
) as the sum
I^{\prime\prime}+II^{\prime\prime}
where

(6.159)

I^{\prime\prime}=\bigg[\frac{-i}{\pi(N-m)}\bigg(\frac{[T_{\infty}(x)]_{11}^{2}\phi(x)\varphi(x)}{s^{\prime}_{N,m}(x)((N-n)s_{N,n}^{\prime}(x)+(N-m)s_{N,m}^{\prime}(x))}\bigg)e^{i((N-n)s_{N,n}(x)+(N-m)s_{N,m}(x))}\bigg]_{-\infty}^{1-N^{-1/3}}

(6.160)

II^{\prime\prime}=\frac{i}{\pi(N-m)}\int_{-\infty}^{1-N^{-1/3}}\frac{d}{dx}\bigg(\frac{[T_{\infty}(x)]_{11}^{2}\phi(x)\varphi(x)}{s^{\prime}_{N,m}(x)((N-n)s_{N,n}^{\prime}(x)+(N-m)s_{N,m}^{\prime}(x))}\bigg)e^{i((N-n)s_{N,n}(x)+(N-m)s_{N,m}(x))}dx.

Observing that
[T_{\infty}(x)]_{11}=O((1-x)^{-1/4})
and that
s^{\prime}(x)=O((1-x)^{1/2})
, we see that there is
C
such that

(6.161)

|I^{\prime}|\leq N^{-2}C|[x^{-3/2}]_{N^{-1/3}}^{\infty}|=CN^{-3/2},\;\;|II^{\prime\prime}|\leq\frac{C}{N^{2}}\int_{N^{-1/3}}^{\infty}\frac{dx}{x^{5/2}}\leq CN^{-3/2}.

Together these show that the
(k,l)=(1,1)
term in (
6.158
) is
O(N^{-3/2})
. Showing that the
(k,l)=(2,2)
is
O(N^{-3/2})
is similar. Now lastly, we will need a sharp form of Lemma
4.4
. For this we note that for
|x|<1
,
s^{\prime}(x)=-\sqrt{1-x^{2}}
, and so for
x\in(1-\delta,1-N^{-1/3})
we have that
s^{\prime\prime}(x)=O(N^{1/6})
. From this, we see that for fixed
k
and
x\in(1-\delta,1-N^{-1/3})
, we have that

(6.162)

(N-k)s_{N,k}(x)=Ns(x)-k\mathrm{arccos}(x)+O(N^{-5/6}).

Employing this and proceeding as in the proof of Lemma
6.6
, we may show now that (
6.158
) coincides with

(6.163)

\frac{1}{2\pi N}\int_{-1}^{1-N^{-1/3}}\frac{\phi(x)\varphi(x)}{(1-x^{2})}\sin((m-n)\mathrm{arccos}(x))dx+O(N^{-7/6})=

(6.164)

\frac{1}{2\pi N}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\phi(x)\varphi(x)}{(1-x^{2})}\sin((m-n)\mathrm{arccos}(x))dx+O(N^{-7/6}).

Altogether, these statements complete the proof of (
6.118
).
∎

Taking a partition of unity with respect to all of the above regions, we may conclude the following proposition.

Proposition 6.11
.

Let
\phi
and
\varphi
be smooth functions of subexponential growth. Then we have that for any
n
and
m
that

(6.165)

\frac{1}{2}\int\int\phi(x)\varphi(y)p_{N-n}(x)p_{N-m}(y)\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}w_{N}(y)^{1/2}dx=

(6.166)

\frac{1}{2\pi N}[\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\phi(x)\varphi(x)}{(1-x^{2})}\sin((m-n)\mathrm{arccos}(x))dx]+

(6.167)

\frac{1}{4N}[(-1)^{N-m}\phi(1)\varphi(-1)-(-1)^{N-n}\phi(-1)\varphi(1)]+O(N^{-7/6}).

We see that as

(6.168)

\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\sin(\mathrm{arccos}(x))}{(1-x^{2})}dx=\pi,

Proposition
6.11
shows that when
N
is even that

(6.169)

\int J_{N}^{-1}(p_{N})(x)p_{N-1}(x)w_{N}(x)dx=O(N^{-7/6}).

For
n
and
m
arbitrary, we additionally see that

(6.170)

\int J_{N}^{-1}(p_{N-n})(x)p_{N-m}(x)w_{N}(x)dx=O(N^{-1}).

Together these demonstrate two of the integrals required by Proposition
2.9
. Before proceeding, we also observe the following corollary of Lemma
6.1
, and the single integral cases of Lemmas
6.6
and
6.10
.

Corollary 6.12
.

Assume that
\phi
is a smooth function on
\mathbb{R}
of sub-exponential growth. Then for fixed
k
, we have that

(6.171)

\int\phi(x)p_{N-k}(x)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}dx=O(N^{-1/2}).

To complete the proof of Proposition
2.9
, we must now focus our attention on the integrals involving
\ell_{i}
and
q_{i}
. We will begin with some single integral computations.

Lemma 6.13
.

Assume that
\phi
is a smooth function of subexponential growth. Then for any
1\leq i\leq m
, we have that

(6.172)

\int\phi(x)\ell_{i}(x)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}dx=O(N^{-1/2}),\;\;\;\int\phi(x)q_{i}(x)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}dx=O(1).

Proof.

We adopt the notation
\lambda=\lambda_{i}
and
\alpha=\alpha_{i}
as before. In view of Proposition
4.14
and (
4.81
) we see that

(6.173)

\int\phi(x)\begin{bmatrix}-\ell_{i}(x)\\
2\pi iq_{i}(x)\end{bmatrix}w_{N}(x)^{1/2}dx=

(6.174)

c_{i}^{-\sigma}e^{-i\frac{\pi}{4}}e^{-\frac{\pi i}{4}\sigma}\sigma_{1}T_{N,\lambda}(\lambda)^{-1}(I+O(N^{-1}))e^{-N\frac{\ell}{2}\sigma}[\int\frac{\phi(x)}{x-\lambda}\begin{bmatrix}\kappa_{N}^{-1}p_{N}(x)\\
-2\pi i\kappa_{N-1}p_{N-1}(x)\end{bmatrix}w_{N}(x)^{1/2}dx].

From Remark
4.6
we see that the right-hand side may further be written as

(6.175)

c_{i}^{-\sigma}e^{-i\frac{\pi}{4}}e^{-\frac{\pi i}{4}\sigma}\sigma_{1}T_{N,\lambda}(\lambda)^{-1}(I+O(N^{-1}))[\int\frac{\phi(x)}{x-\lambda}\begin{bmatrix}\pi^{1/2}p_{N}(x)\\
-i\pi^{1/2}p_{N-1}(x)\end{bmatrix}w_{N}(x)^{1/2}dx].

Thus we see that it suffices to show that

(6.176)

\int\frac{\phi(x)}{x-\lambda}\begin{bmatrix}\pi^{1/2}p_{N}(x)\\
-i\pi^{1/2}p_{N-1}(x)\end{bmatrix}w_{N}(x)^{1/2}dx=T_{N,\lambda}(\lambda)\begin{bmatrix}O(1)\\
0\end{bmatrix}+O(N^{-1/2}).

Let us pick
\delta_{0}>0
such that asymptotics of Proposition
4.13
hold. By applying Corollary
6.12
we see that it suffices to assume that
\phi
is supported on
(\lambda-\delta_{0},\lambda+\delta_{0})
. With the asymptotics of Proposition
4.13
, and a change of coordinates by
f_{\lambda}
, we may write

(6.177)

\int\frac{\phi(x)}{x-\lambda}\begin{bmatrix}\pi^{1/2}p_{N}(x)\\
-i\pi^{1/2}p_{N-1}(x)\end{bmatrix}w_{N}(x)^{1/2}dx=

(6.178)

\int\frac{G_{N,1}(x)}{x}\mathrm{Sign}(x)\sqrt{\pi|Nx|}J_{\alpha+1/2}(N|x|)dx+\int\frac{G_{N,2}(x)}{x}\sqrt{\pi|Nx|}J_{\alpha-1/2}(N|x|)dx=I+II,

where here
G_{N,i}=(f_{\lambda}^{-1})^{\prime}(x)\phi(f_{\lambda}^{-1}(x))[(I+R_{N}(f_{\lambda}^{-1}(x)))T_{N,\lambda}(f_{\lambda}^{-1}(x))]e_{i}
. We observe that
G_{N,i}^{(c)}(x)=O(1)
uniformly for all
c\in\mathbb{N}
. From this, we see by Lemma
6.7
that

(6.179)

I=2\sqrt{\pi}G_{N,1}(0)D(\alpha+1/2,-1)+O(N^{-1})=\sqrt{\pi}(f_{\lambda}^{-1})^{\prime}(0)\phi(\lambda)[T_{N,\lambda}(\lambda)]e_{1}D(\alpha+1/2,-1/2)+O(N^{-1}).

The case of
II
is similar except that the first-order contribution of the integrals on
(0,\infty)
and
(-\infty,0)
cancel so that
II=O(N^{-1})
. Together these computations show that

(6.180)

\int\frac{\phi(x)}{x-\lambda}\begin{bmatrix}\pi^{1/2}p_{N}(x)\\
-i\pi^{1/2}p_{N-1}(x)\end{bmatrix}w_{N}(x)^{1/2}dx=T_{N,\lambda}(\lambda)\begin{bmatrix}\phi(\lambda)\sqrt{\pi}2D(\alpha+1/2,-1/2)\\
0\end{bmatrix}+O(N^{-1}),

which is more than sufficient
to verify (
6.176
).
∎

We recall from Remark
4.15
that for fixed
1\leq i\leq m
there are constants
C_{1,N},C_{2,N}
with
C_{k,N}=O(1)
and such that

(6.181)

\ell_{i}(x)=C_{1,N}\frac{p_{N}(x)}{x-\lambda_{i}}+C_{2,N}\frac{p_{N-1}(x)}{x-\lambda_{i}},

and similarly for
q_{i}
. Let us take
\delta
as above. We fix for, each
1\leq l\leq m
, a choice of smooth function
\phi_{l}
supported on
(\lambda_{l}-\delta/2,\lambda_{l}+\delta/2)
, such that
\phi_{l}(x)=1
for
x\in(\lambda_{l}-\delta/4,\lambda_{l}+\delta/4)
. In addition, we choose
\varphi_{l}
supported on
(\lambda_{l}-\delta,\lambda_{l}+\delta)
, such that
\varphi_{l}(x)=1
for
x\in(\lambda_{l}-\delta/2,\lambda_{l}+\delta/2)
.

With these selected, we may write

(6.182)

\int J_{N}^{-1}\ell_{i}(x)p_{N-n}(x)w_{N}(x)dx=\int(J_{N}^{-1}\phi_{i}\ell_{i})(x)p_{N-n}(x)w_{N}(x)dx+\int(J_{N}^{-1}(1-\phi_{i})\ell_{i})(x)p_{N-n}(x)w_{N}(x)dx.

Employing (
6.181
), we may write the second integral as

(6.183)

\sum_{k=0,1}\frac{C_{k+1,N}}{2}\int\int p_{N-n}(x)p_{N-k}(y)\frac{(1-\phi_{i}(y))}{(y-\lambda_{i})}w_{N}(x)w_{N}(y)dxdy.

Observing that the function
(1-\phi_{i}(x))/(x-\lambda_{i})
is smooth and bounded, we may apply Proposition
6.11
to see that the terms in (
6.183
) are of order
O(N^{-1})
. From this, we see that

(6.184)

\int J_{N}^{-1}\ell_{i}(x)p_{N-n}(x)w_{N}(x)dx=\int(J_{N}^{-1}\phi_{i}\ell_{i})(x)p_{N-n}(x)w_{N}(x)dx+O(N^{-1}).

We now also write

(6.185)

\int(J_{N}^{-1}\phi_{i}\ell_{i})(x)p_{N-n}(x)w_{N}(x)dx=

(6.186)

\int(J_{N}^{-1}\phi_{i}\ell_{i})(x)p_{N-n}(x)\varphi_{i}(x)w_{N}(x)dx+\int(J_{N}^{-1}(\phi_{i}\ell_{i}))(x)(1-\varphi_{i}(x))p_{N-n}(x)w_{N}(x)dx.

We note that as
\phi_{i}\ell_{i}
and
(1-\varphi)
have disjoint support, we may expand
J_{N}^{-1}
and employ (
6.2
) to rewrite the second integral on the right-hand side as

(6.187)

\frac{1}{2}\int\phi_{i}(y)\ell_{i}(y)w_{N}(y)^{1/2}dy\int(1-\varphi_{i}(x))\mathrm{Sign}(x-\lambda_{i})p_{N-n}(x)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}dx

which is
O(N^{-1})
by employing Lemma
6.13
and Corollary
6.12
. Altogether we see that

(6.188)

\int J_{N}^{-1}\ell_{i}(x)p_{N-n}(x)w_{N}(x)dx=\int J_{N}^{-1}(\phi_{i}\ell_{i})(x)\varphi_{i}(x)p_{N-n}(x)w_{N}(x)dx+O(N^{-1}).

Similarly, we obtain that

(6.189)

\int J_{N}^{-1}p_{N-n}(x)q_{i}(x)w_{N}(x)dx=\int J_{N}^{-1}(\phi_{i}p_{N-n})(x)\varphi_{i}(x)q_{i}(x)w_{N}(x)dx+O(N^{-1/2}),

(6.190)

\int J_{N}^{-1}\ell_{i}(x)q_{j}(x)w_{N}(x)dx=\delta_{ij}\int J_{N}^{-1}(\phi_{i}\ell_{i})(x)\varphi_{i}(x)q_{i}(x)w_{N}(x)dx+O(N^{-1/2}).

From these computations and (
6.181
), we see that to complete the proof of Proposition
2.9
, it suffices to show the following.

Lemma 6.14
.

There is
\delta>0
such that if
\phi
and
\varphi
are smooth functions supported on
(\lambda_{j}-\delta,\lambda_{j}+\delta)
for some
1\leq j\leq m
then for any choice of fixed
n
and
m
we have that

(6.191)

\frac{1}{2}\int\int\frac{\phi(x)p_{N-n}(x)}{x-\lambda_{j}}\varphi(y)p_{N-m}(y)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}w_{N}(y)^{1/2}dxdy=O(N^{-1}\log(N)).

Moreover, if
|n-m|\leq 1
, then we additionally have that

(6.192)

\frac{1}{2}\int\int\frac{\phi(x)p_{N-n}(x)}{x-\lambda_{j}}\frac{\varphi(y)p_{N-m}(y)}{y-\lambda_{j}}w_{N}(x)w_{N}(y)dxdy=O(N^{-1}\log(N)).

Proof.

Again, for convenience, we write
\lambda=\lambda_{j}
and
\alpha=\alpha_{j}
, and choose
\delta
as before. We begin by proving the first statement. With notation as in Lemma
6.6
, we may rewrite the left hand side of (
6.191
) as in (
6.61
) as

(6.193)

\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{k,l=1,2}\int\int\frac{1}{x-\lambda}h_{N,k}(x)[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(g_{N,n}(x))]_{k}l_{N,l}(y)[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(g_{N,m}(y))]_{l}\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)dxdy.

We claim that the summand for each choice of
(k,l)
is
O(N^{-1}\log(N))
. To begin, we define, for
i=1,2
, the functions
I_{i}(x)=\frac{1}{2}\int\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(y)]_{i}y^{-1}dy
. We note that by performing integration by parts in
x
(see the proof of Lemma
6.5
) we may write (
6.193
) as the sum
I+II
where

(6.194)

I=\frac{1}{\pi}\sum_{k,l=1,2}\int[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(g_{N,m}(x))]_{l}I_{k}(g_{N,n}(x))\frac{h_{N,k}(x)l_{N,l}(x)g_{N,n}(x)}{g_{N,n}^{\prime}(x)(x-\lambda)}dx,

(6.195)

II=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{k,l=1,2}\int\int[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(g_{N,m}(x))]_{l}I_{k}(g_{N,n}(y))l_{N,l}(y)\frac{d}{dx}(\frac{h_{N,k}(x)g_{N,n}(x)}{g_{N,n}^{\prime}(x)(x-\lambda)})\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)dxdy.

We observe that as
g_{N,n}(\lambda)=0
we have that
g_{N,n}(x)/((x-\lambda)g_{N,n}^{\prime}(x))
as well as its derivatives are
O(1)
for
x\in(\lambda-\delta,\lambda+\delta)
. We note that
I_{1}(x)
(
I_{2}(x)
) is anti-symmetric (symmetric), respectively. Moreover, for
x>0
, we have the relations

(6.196)

I_{1}(x)=\int_{0}^{x}[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(y)]_{1}y^{-1}dy=\sqrt{\pi}J_{\mu+1,-1/2}(x),

(6.197)

I_{2}(x)=\int_{x}^{\infty}[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(y)]_{2}y^{-1}dy=\sqrt{\pi}\int_{x}^{\infty}y^{-1/2}J_{\alpha+1/2}(y)dy.

Applying the asymptotics (
6.67
) and (
5.27
) above, it follows that for
i=1,2
we have that
I_{i}(x)=O(|x|^{-1})
for
|x|\geq 1
,
I_{i}(x)=O(1)
for
|x|\leq 1
, and
[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(x)]_{l}=O(1)
for all
x
. Applying these bounds pointwise, and noting that
h_{N,k}(x),l_{N,l}(x)=O(1)
, we see that the integrand of
I
is uniformly
O(N^{-1}|x|^{-1})
for
|x|\geq 1/N
, and
O(1)
for
|x|\leq 1/N
. Thus contribution of the integral over
\mathbb{R}\setminus(\lambda-N^{-1},\lambda+N^{-1})
is of order
O(N^{-1}\log(N))
and the contribution over
(\lambda-N^{-1},\lambda+N^{-1})
is of order
O(N^{-1})
, so that
I=O(N^{-1}\log(N))
. Similarly we see that
II=O(N^{-1}\log(N))
. Together these establish (
6.191
).

We now establish (
6.192
). The key difference, in this case, is that we may employ Proposition
4.13
only once to obtain the asymptotics of both
p_{N-n}
and
p_{N-m}
. For notational ease, we will only prove the case when
n=0
and
m=0,1
. We write
h_{N,k}(x)=\phi(x)[(I+R_{N}(x))T_{N,\lambda}(x)]_{1k}
and
l_{N,k}(x)=i^{I(m=1)}\varphi(x)[(I+R_{N}(x))T_{N,\lambda}(x)]_{(1+m)k}
, where
R_{N}
is as in Proposition
4.13
. This is where we use the assumption that
|n-m|\leq 1
so that we may write both terms with the same asymptotic. Using this, we may rewrite the left-hand side of (
6.192
) as

(6.198)

\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{k,l=1,2}\int\int\frac{h_{N,k}(x)l_{N,l}(y)}{(x-\lambda)(y-\lambda)}[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(Nf_{\lambda}(x))]_{k}[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(Nf_{\lambda}(y))]_{l}\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)dxdy.

If we define

(6.199)

\bar{h}_{N,i}(x)=h_{N,i}(f_{\lambda}^{-1}(x))\frac{(f_{\lambda}^{-1})^{\prime}(x)x}{(f_{\lambda}^{-1}(x)-\lambda)},

and similarly for
\bar{l}_{N,i}
, we may further rewrite (
6.192
) as

(6.200)

\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{k,l=1,2}\int\int(\frac{\bar{h}_{N,k}(x)\bar{l}_{N,l}(y)}{xy})[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(Nx)]_{k}[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(Ny)]_{l}\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)dxdy.

Let us fix
\chi(x)
, a smooth, even, function of compact support, with
\chi(x)=1
for
x\in(-\delta,\delta)
. Applying Taylor’s theorem to
\bar{h}_{N,l}
and
\bar{l}_{N,l}
and proceeding as in the proof of (
6.191
), we see that that it suffices to show that for
i=1,2
and
j=1,2
, that

(6.201)

\frac{1}{2}\int\int\frac{\chi(x)\chi(y)}{xy}[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(Nx)]_{i}[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(Ny)]_{j}\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)dxdy=O(N^{-1}\log(N)).

This integral is antisymmetric under the interchange of
i
and
j
. Thus it suffices to prove the case of
(i,j)=(1,2)
. By employing Lemma
6.5
we may rewrite (
6.201
) in the case
(i,j)=(1,2)
as the sum of three integrals
I+II+III

(6.202)

I=\int x^{-1}[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(Nx)]_{1}I_{2}(Nx)\chi(x)\chi(x)dx,

(6.203)

II=\int I_{1}(Nx)I_{2}(Nx)\chi(x)\chi^{\prime}(x)dx,

(6.204)

III=\frac{1}{2}\int\int I_{1}(Nx)I_{2}(Ny)\chi^{\prime}(x)\chi^{\prime}(y)\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)dxdy.

As
\chi^{\prime}
vanishes in a neighborhood of
0
, and
I_{i}(x)=O(|x|^{-1})
, we see that
II,III=O(N^{-2})
. To deal with
I
, we first rewrite it as

(6.205)

I=\int x^{-1}[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(x)]_{1}I_{2}(x)\chi(x/N)^{2}dx.

As
[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(x)]_{1}=O(1)
, we note that
x^{-1}[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(x)]_{1}I_{2}(x)=O(|x|^{-2})
for
x\in\mathbb{R}\setminus(-1,1)
, and
O(1)
for
x\in[-1,1]
so that
x^{-1}[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(x)]_{1}I_{2}(x)
is integrable. In addition, we see that there is
C
such that

(6.206)

|\int x^{-1}[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(x)]_{1}I_{2}(x)(1-\chi(x/N)^{2})dx|\leq C\int_{|x|\geq\delta N}x^{-2}dx=2C\delta^{-1}N^{-1}.

Combining these, we see that

(6.207)

I=\int x^{-1}[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(x)]_{1}I_{2}(x)dx+O(N^{-1})=2\sqrt{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}x^{-1/2}J_{\alpha+1/2}(x)I_{2}(x)dx+O(N^{-1}).

We write

(6.208)

I^{\prime}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}\int_{0}^{\infty}x^{-1/2}J_{\alpha+1/2}(x)I_{2}(x)dx=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{x}^{\infty}x^{-1/2}J_{\alpha+1/2}(x)y^{-1/2}J_{\alpha-1/2}(y)dydx,

we now need only show that
I^{\prime}=0
. Making the change of variables
y=ax
, this integral becomes

(6.209)

\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{1}^{\infty}a^{-1/2}J_{\alpha+1/2}(x)J_{\alpha-1/2}(ax)dadx.

We note that by equation 11.4.41 of
[
1
]
, we have that

(6.210)

\int_{0}^{\infty}a^{-1/2}J_{\alpha+1/2}(x)J_{\alpha-1/2}(ax)dx=0;\;\;\;a>1.

This shows that (
6.209
) vanishes when the integrals are taken in the opposite order. As the integrals are not absolutely convergent, though, we must proceed with care. Applying the above bounds on
I_{2}
and
J_{\alpha+1/2}
, we have that

(6.211)

\int_{0}^{L}\int_{L}^{\infty}a^{-1/2}J_{\alpha+1/2}(x)J_{\alpha-1/2}(ax)dadx=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}\int_{0}^{L}x^{-1/2}J_{\alpha+1/2}(x)I_{2}(Lx)dx=O(L^{-1}\log(L)).

Thus we have that

(6.212)

I^{\prime}=\lim_{L\to\infty}\int_{0}^{L}\int_{1}^{\infty}a^{-1/2}J_{\alpha+1/2}(x)J_{\alpha-1/2}(ax)dadx=\lim_{L\to\infty}\int_{0}^{L}\int_{1}^{L}a^{-1/2}J_{\alpha+1/2}(x)J_{\alpha-1/2}(ax)dadx.

By employing (
6.30
) and the double-angle formula, we may compute that

(6.213)

\int_{1}^{L}\int_{L}^{\infty}a^{-1/2}J_{\alpha+1/2}(x)J_{\alpha-1/2}(ax)dxda=

(6.214)

-\frac{2}{\pi}\int_{1}^{L}\int_{L}^{\infty}\bigg(\frac{\sin(x+\alpha\frac{\pi}{2})\cos(ax+\alpha\frac{\pi}{2})}{xa}+O(x^{-2}a^{-1})\bigg)dxda=

(6.215)

-\frac{2}{\pi}\int_{1}^{L}\frac{1}{2a}\bigg[\int_{L}^{\infty}\ sin((1+a)x+\alpha\pi)x^{-1}dx-\int_{L}^{\infty}\sin((a-1)x)x^{-1}dx\bigg]da+O(\log(L)L^{-1}).

We note that there is
C
such that for
y>0
, we have that
\int_{y}^{\infty}\sin(x)x^{-1}dx\leq Cy^{-1}
. Employing this, we see that

(6.216)

|\int_{1}^{L}\frac{1}{a}\int_{L}^{\infty}\sin((1+a)x+\alpha\pi)x^{-1}dxda|\leq C^{2}\int_{1}^{L}\frac{1}{aL}da=O(L^{-1}\log(L)).

We also note that there is
C
such that for
y>0

(6.217)

|\int_{y}^{\infty}\sin(x)x^{-1}dx|\leq C.

From this, we see that

(6.218)

|\int_{1}^{L}\frac{1}{a}\int_{L}^{\infty}\sin((a-1)x)x^{-1}dxda|\leq|\int_{1}^{1+L^{-1}}\frac{1}{a}\int_{L(a-1)}^{\infty}\sin(x)x^{-1}dxda|+

(6.219)

|\int_{1+L^{-1}}^{L}\frac{1}{a}\int_{L(a-1)}^{\infty}\sin(x)x^{-1}dxda|\leq C|\int_{1+L^{-1}}^{L}\frac{1}{La(a-1)}dxda|+C|\int_{1+L^{-1}}^{L}\frac{1}{a}da|.

The first integral on the right-most side of (
6.219
) is of order
O(L^{-1})
and the second is of order
O(L^{-1}\log(L))
. Altogether we see that

(6.220)

\lim_{L\to\infty}\int_{1}^{L}a^{-1/2}\int_{L}^{\infty}J_{\alpha+1/2}(x)J_{\alpha-1/2}(ax)dxda=0.

Thus finally

(6.221)

\lim_{L\to\infty}\int_{0}^{L}\int_{1}^{L}a^{-1/2}J_{\alpha+1/2}(x)J_{\alpha-1/2}(ax)dadx=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{1}^{\infty}a^{-1/2}J_{\alpha+1/2}(x)J_{\alpha-1/2}(ax)dadx=0.

This shows that
I^{\prime}=0
which completes the proof of (
6.191
).
∎

7
Proof of Proposition
2.13

In this section, we will provide a proof of Proposition
2.13
. The method of proof will be almost identical in structure to the proof of Proposition
2.9
given in the last section. The main differences that occur are that we must now choose
N
-dependent partitions of unity to isolate the regions of each specified asymptotics and that now the error asymptotics of
R_{N}
are of order
O(N^{\gamma-1})
in the regions around
\{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\}
. We will now, for the remainder of this section, assume that we are in the case of Proposition
2.13
. That is, we will fix
1>\gamma>0
and
\epsilon>0
, and only consider choices of (possibly
N
-dependant)
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\in(-1+\epsilon,1-\epsilon)
such that
(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})>N^{-\gamma}
.

We will first focus on integral results in the region
(\lambda_{2}-\delta,\lambda_{1}+\delta)
. Let us fix
\epsilon,\delta_{0}>0
as in Proposition
4.17
, and
\delta<\min(\epsilon/4,1/4,\delta_{0}/2)
. Let us denote
\delta_{N}=\delta(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})
. We will fix a symmetric smooth function
\chi
, such that
\chi(x)=1
for
|x|<1/8
and
\chi(x)=0
for
|x|>1/4
. We define
\chi_{N,i}(x)=\chi((x-\lambda_{i})\delta_{N}^{-1})
.
We additionally choose another smooth compactly-supported function
\bar{\chi}
, supported on
(\lambda_{2}-\delta/2,\lambda_{1}+\delta/2)
, such that
\bar{\chi}(x)=1
for
x\in(\lambda_{2}-\delta/4,\lambda_{1}+\delta/4)
. We define
\chi_{N,0}(x)=\bar{\chi}(x)-\chi_{N,1}(x)-\chi_{N,2}(x)
. A key property of these functions is the following: for
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
i\in\{0,1,2\}
, there is
C
such that

(7.1)

\int|\chi_{N,i}^{(k)}(x)|dx\leq C(\delta_{N}^{-k+1}+\delta_{ik}\delta_{k0})\leq C^{2}(N^{\gamma(k-1)}+\delta_{ik}\delta_{k0}),\;\;\sup_{x}|\chi^{(k)}_{N,i}(x)|\leq C\delta_{N}^{-k}\leq C^{2}N^{\gamma k}.

In addition, we will define
\chi_{N,i,j}(x)=\chi_{N,i}(x)/(x-\lambda_{j})
. An important subtlety that arises in the current context is that for
i\neq j
, the function
\chi_{N,i,j}(x)
is no longer
O(1)
. On the other hand, we instead observe that for
i\neq j
, and
k\in\mathbb{N}
, there is
C>0
, such that

(7.2)

\int|\chi_{N,i,j}^{(k)}(x)|dx\leq C(\delta_{N}^{-k}+\log(\delta_{N})\delta_{ik}\delta_{k0})\leq C^{2}(N^{\gamma k}+\gamma\log(N)\delta_{ik}\delta_{k0}),

(7.3)

\sup_{x}|\chi_{N,i,j}^{(k)}(x)|\leq C\delta_{N}^{-k-1}\leq C^{2}N^{\gamma(k+1)}.

The key property we will use for
\chi_{N,i}
and
\chi_{N,i,j}
will be (
7.1
) and (
7.3
), respectively, the majority of proofs of the results below are identical for both functions, with slightly worse bounds for the latter. We begin our analysis with the following modification of Lemma
6.1
.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
\phi
be a smooth function. Then for each fixed
k\in\mathbb{Z}
,
i=1,2
, and choice of
c\in\mathbb{N}
, we have that

(7.4)

\int\phi(x)\chi_{N,0}(x)p_{N-k}(x)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}dx=O(N^{-c}),

(7.5)

\int\phi(x)\chi_{N,0,i}(x)p_{N-k}(x)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}dx=O(N^{-c}).

Proof.

We may assume that
c>1
. We first establish (
7.4
). For notational clarity, we assume that
k=0
. We define
f_{N,l}
as in Lemma
6.1
. By (
6.8
) we see that for
c\in\mathbb{N}
,

(7.6)

|\int\phi(x)\chi_{N,0}(x)p_{N-k}(x)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}dx|\leq\frac{1}{\pi^{1/2}}\sum_{l=1,2}\frac{1}{N^{c}}\int|\frac{d^{c}}{dy^{c}}((s^{-1})^{\prime}(y)\chi_{N,0}(s^{-1}(y))f_{N,l}(s^{-1}(y)))|dy.

We observe that
f_{N,l}^{(c)}(x)=O(1)
for
c\in\mathbb{N}
. By the product formula, we may inductively write

(7.7)

\frac{d^{c}}{dy^{c}}((s^{-1})^{\prime}(y)\chi_{N,0}(s^{-1}(y))f_{N,l}(s^{-1}(y)))=\sum_{k=0}^{c}\chi_{N,0}^{(k)}(s^{-1}(y))a_{k,l,N}(y),

where
a_{k,l,N}(y)
are smooth functions with
a_{k,l,N}(y)=O(1)
uniformly. Thus we have that there is
C
such that

(7.8)

\int|\frac{d^{c}}{dy^{c}}((s^{-1})^{\prime}(y)\chi_{N,0}(s^{-1}(y))f_{N,l}(s^{-1}(y)))|dy\leq\sum_{k=0}^{c}\int|\chi_{N,0}^{(k)}(y)a_{k,l,N}(s(y))s^{\prime}(y)|dy\leq

(7.9)

C\sum_{k=0}^{c}\int|\chi_{N,0}^{(k)}(y)|dy\leq C^{2}N^{c\gamma},

where in the second inequality, we have used (
7.1
). In particular, we see that

(7.10)

\int\phi(x)\chi_{N,0}(x)p_{N-k}(x)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}dx=O(N^{c(\gamma-1)}).

As
c\in\mathbb{N}
was arbitrary, we see that taking
c\mapsto\lceil c/(1-\gamma)\rceil
, we obtain (
7.4
).

To show (
7.5
), the above proof works, replacing (
7.1
) for (
7.3
) to show that the left-hand side of (
7.5
) is
O(N^{(c+1)\gamma-c})
. Adjusting
c
again gives (
7.5
).
∎

We now state the modification of Lemma
6.2
that we shall use.

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
\phi
and
\varphi
be smooth functions. Then for fixed choice of
n
and
m
and
i,j\in\{1,2\}
, we have that

(7.11)

\frac{1}{2}\int\int\phi(x)\varphi(y)\chi_{N,0}(x)\chi_{N,0}(y)p_{N-n}(x)p_{N-m}(y)\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}w_{N}(y)^{1/2}dxdy=

(7.12)

\frac{1}{2\pi N}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\phi(x)\varphi(x)\chi_{N,0}(x)^{2}}{(1-x^{2})}\sin((m-n)\mathrm{arccos}(x))dx+O(N^{\gamma-2}),

(7.13)

\int\int\phi(x)\varphi(y)\chi_{N,0,i}(x)\chi_{N,0}(y)p_{N-n}(x)p_{N-m}(y)\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}w_{N}(y)^{1/2}dxdy=O(N^{-1}),

(7.14)

\int\int\phi(x)\varphi(y)\chi_{N,0,i}(x)\chi_{N,0,j}(y)p_{N-n}(x)p_{N-m}(y)\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}w_{N}(y)^{1/2}dxdy=

(7.15)

O(N^{-\min(1,2(1-\gamma))}).

Proof.

We begin with the proof of (
7.12
). As in the proof of Lemma
6.2
, we write

(7.16)

\frac{1}{2}\int\int\phi(x)\varphi(y)\chi_{N,0}(x)\chi_{N,0}(y)p_{N-n}(x)p_{N-m}(y)\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}w_{N}(y)^{1/2}dxdy=

(7.17)

\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{j,k=1,2}\int\int\chi_{N,0}(x)\chi_{N,0}(y)f_{N,j,k}(x,y)e^{-(-1)^{j}Nis(x)-(-1)^{k}Nis(y)}\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)dxdy,

where here
f_{N,j,k}
is defined as in the proof of Lemma
6.2
. We denote

(7.18)

\bar{f}_{N,j,k}(x,y)=(s^{-1})^{\prime}(x)(s^{-1})^{\prime}(y)f_{N,j,k}(s^{-1}(x),s^{-1}(y)).

We have that for
c\in\mathbb{N}
that
\bar{f}_{N,j,k}^{(c)}(x)=O(1)
uniformly in
x
. One may apply Lemma
6.3
as in the proof of Lemma
6.2
to write

(7.19)

\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j,k=1,2}\int\int\chi_{N,0}(x)\chi_{N,0}(y)f_{N,j,k}(x,y)e^{-(-1)^{j}Nis(x)-(-1)^{k}Nis(y)}\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)dxdy=

(7.20)

-\frac{i}{N}\int\frac{f_{N,1,2}(x,x)\chi_{N,0}(x)^{2}}{|s^{\prime}(x)|}dx+\frac{i}{N}\int\frac{f_{N,2,1}(x,x)\chi_{N,0}(x)^{2}}{|s^{\prime}(x)|}dx+E_{N},

where
E_{N}
is bounded by

(7.21)

\displaystyle\sum_{j,k=1,2}\frac{4}{N^{2}}\bigg(\int\int\|\nabla(\chi_{N,0}(s^{-1}(x))\chi_{N,0}(s^{-1}(y))\bar{f}_{N,j,k}(x,y))\|dxdy+

(7.22)

\displaystyle 4\int|\frac{d}{dx}(\chi_{N,0}(s^{-1}(x))\chi_{N,0}(s^{-1}(x))\bar{f}_{N,j,k}(x,x))|dx\bigg).

As in the proof of Lemma
7.1
, and recalling that
\|(x,y)\|\leq|x|+|y|
, we see that there is
C
such that (
7.22
) is bounded by

(7.23)

CN^{-2}(\int|\chi_{N,0}^{\prime}(x)|dx\int|\chi_{N,0}(y)|dy+\int|\chi_{N,0}(x)|dx\int|\chi_{N,0}(y)|dy+

(7.24)

\int|\chi_{N,0}^{\prime}(x)||\chi_{N,0}(x)|dx+\int|\chi_{N,0}(x)|^{2}dx).

We further note that

(7.25)

\int|\chi_{N,0}^{\prime}(x)||\chi_{N,0}(x)|dx+\int|\chi_{N,0}(x)|^{2}dx\leq(\sup_{x}|\chi_{N,0}(x)|)(\int|\chi_{N,0}^{\prime}(x)|dx+\int|\chi_{N,0}(x)|dx).

All of these terms are
O(1)
by (
7.1
). Thus we see that we have that
E_{N}=O(N^{-2})
. As we have that
R_{N}(x),\bar{R}_{N}(x)=

O(N^{\gamma-1})
we have that

(7.26)

f_{N,j,k}(x,y)=\phi(x)\varphi(y)[T_{\infty}(x)]_{1j}[T_{\infty}(y)]_{1k}e^{(-1)^{j}i(n\mathrm{arccos}(x)+\pi(I(x<\lambda_{1})+I(x<\lambda_{2}))\alpha)}\times

(7.27)

e^{(-1)^{k}i(m\mathrm{arccos}(y)+\pi(I(y<\lambda_{1})+I(y<\lambda_{2})))\alpha}+O(N^{\gamma-1}).

The remainder of the proof of (
7.12
) consists of inserting (
7.27
) into (
7.20
) and simplifying the resulting expression identically to as in the proof of Lemma
6.2
.

To establish (
7.13
), the entire proof works identically, except that
E_{N}
is now bounded by

(7.28)

CN^{-2}[\int|\chi_{N,0,i}^{\prime}(x)|dx\int|\chi_{N,0}(x)|dx+\int|\chi_{N,0}^{\prime}(x)|dx\int|\chi_{N,0,i}(x)|dx+

(7.29)

\int|\chi_{N,0}(x)|dx\int|\chi_{N,0,i}(x)|dx+(\sup_{x}|\chi_{N,0}(x)|)(\int|\chi_{N,0,i}^{\prime}(x)|dx+\int|\chi_{N,0,i}(x)|dx)+

(7.30)

(\sup_{x}|\chi_{N,0}(x)|)\int|\chi_{N,0,i}^{\prime}(x)|dx],

for some
C>0
. Employing (
7.1
) and (
7.3
) we see that
E_{N}=O(N^{\gamma-2})
, which establishes (
7.13
).

In the case of (
7.15
),
we have that
E_{N}
is now bounded by

(7.31)

CN^{-2}[\int|\chi_{N,0,i}^{\prime}(x)|dx\int|\chi_{N,0,j}(x)|dx+\int|\chi_{N,0,j}^{\prime}(x)|dx\int|\chi_{N,0,i}(x)|dx+

(7.32)

\int|\chi_{N,0,j}(x)|dx\int|\chi_{N,0,i}(x)|dx+(\sup_{x}|\chi_{N,0,j}(x)|)(\int|\chi_{N,0,i}^{\prime}(x)|dx+\int|\chi_{N,0,i}(x)|dx)+

(7.33)

(\sup_{x}|\chi_{N,0,j}(x)|)\int|\chi_{N,0,i}^{\prime}(x)|dx],

for some
C
. Again employing (
7.3
) we now see that
E_{N}=O(N^{2\gamma-2})
, which establishes (
7.15
).
∎

We now will focus on the modifications that need to be made in the region around
\lambda_{i}
. We note that the preparatory Lemma
6.4
still holds as stated.

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
\phi
and
\varphi
be smooth functions. Then for any
i,j\in\{0,1,2\}
, and fixed
n
and
m
we have that

(7.34)

\int\phi(x)\chi_{N,i}(x)p_{N-n}(x)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}dx=O(N^{-1}),

(7.35)

\frac{1}{2}\int\int\phi(x)\varphi(y)\chi_{N,i}(x)\chi_{N,j}(y)p_{N-n}(x)p_{N-m}(y)\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}w_{N}(y)^{1/2}dxdy=

(7.36)

\frac{1}{2\pi N}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\phi(x)\varphi(x)\chi_{N,i}(x)\chi_{N,j}(x)}{(1-x^{2})}\sin((m-n)\mathrm{arccos}(x))dx+O(N^{\gamma-2}).

In addition, with
r,t=1,2
, such that
i\neq r
and
j\neq t
, we have that

(7.37)

\int\phi(x)\chi_{N,i,r}(x)p_{N-n}(x)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}dx=O(N^{-\min(1,2(1-\gamma))}),

(7.38)

\int\int\phi(x)\varphi(y)\chi_{N,i,r}(x)\chi_{N,j}(y)p_{N-n}(x)p_{N-m}(y)\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}w_{N}(y)^{1/2}dxdy=O(N^{-1}),

(7.39)

\int\int\phi(x)\varphi(y)\chi_{N,i,r}(x)\chi_{N,j,t}(y)p_{N-n}(x)p_{N-m}(y)\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}w_{N}(y)^{1/2}dxdy=O(N^{-\min(1,2(1-\gamma))}).

Proof.

We begin with the proof of (
7.34
). We have already established the case of
i=0
, so we assume that
i>0
, and denote
\lambda_{i}=\lambda
. We observe that proceeding identically to Lemma
6.6
we have that there is
C
such that

(7.40)

|\int_{\lambda}^{\lambda\pm\delta}\phi(x)p_{N-n}(x)\chi_{N,i}(x)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}dx|\leq N^{-1}\pi^{1/2}(D(\alpha+1/2,1/2)|g_{N,1}(\lambda)|+D(\alpha-1/2,1/2)|g_{N,2}(\lambda)|)\times

(7.41)

(f_{N,n,\lambda}^{-1})^{\prime}(0)+\frac{C}{N^{2}}\sum_{k=1,2}\sum_{l=0}^{2}|\int_{0}^{\pm\infty}\frac{d^{l}}{dx^{l}}((f_{N,n,\lambda}^{-1})^{\prime}(x)g_{N,k}(f_{N,n,\lambda}^{-1}(x))\chi_{N,i}(f_{N,n,\lambda}^{-1}(x)))|dx,

where
g_{N,k}
is defined as in the proof of Lemma
6.6
. The first term on the right-hand side is clearly
O(N^{-1})
. Additionally, we may show, as before, that there is
C
, such that the second term of the right-hand side of (
7.41
) may be bounded by

(7.42)

\frac{C^{2}}{N^{2}}\sum_{l=0}^{2}\int|\chi_{N,i}^{(l)}(x)|dx=O(N^{\gamma-2}).

Together these bounds establish (
7.34
). The same argument may be used to establish (
7.37
).

To show (
7.36
), we first observe that the case of
i=j=0
was established in Lemma
7.2
. In the case that
i>0
and
j=0
, let us write
\hat{\chi}(x)=\chi(4x)
and
\hat{\chi}_{N,i}(x)=\hat{\chi}((x-\lambda_{i})\delta_{N}^{-1})
, so that
\text{supp}(\hat{\chi}_{N,i})\cap\text{supp}(\chi_{N,0})=\varnothing
. Thus we see that

(7.43)

\int\int\phi(x)\varphi(y)\hat{\chi}_{N,i}(x)\chi_{N,0}(y)p_{N-n}(x)p_{N-m}(y)\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}w_{N}(y)^{1/2}dxdy=

(7.44)

\int\phi(x)\hat{\chi}_{N,i}(x)p_{N-n}(x)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}dx\int\varphi(y)\chi_{N,0}(y)p_{N-m}(y)\mathrm{Sign}(\lambda_{i}-y)w_{N}(y)^{1/2}dy.

We note that as
\hat{\chi}_{N,i}
clearly satisfies the bounds (
7.1
) and has a support contained within that of
\chi_{N,i}
, the above proof of (
7.34
) with
\chi_{N,i}
replaced by
\hat{\chi}_{N,i}
shows that

(7.45)

\int\phi(x)\hat{\chi}_{N,i}(x)p_{N-n}(x)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}dx=O(N^{-1}).

We also observe that while Lemma
7.1
does not formally apply to the second integral on the right-hand side of (
7.44
), as the function
\phi(x)\mathrm{Sign}(\lambda_{i}-x)
is
N
-dependant though
\lambda_{i}
, this function (as well as its derivatives of up to any finite order) are still uniformly bounded on the support of
\chi_{N,0}
, so that the proof of Lemma
7.1
with
\phi(x)
replaced by
\phi(x)\mathrm{Sign}(\lambda_{i}-x)
shows that for any
c>0
we also have that

(7.46)

\int\varphi(y)\chi_{N,0}(y)p_{N-m}(y)\mathrm{Sign}(\lambda_{i}-y)w_{N}(y)^{1/2}dy=O(N^{-c}).

Together these results show that

(7.47)

\int\int\phi(x)\varphi(y)\hat{\chi}_{N,i}(x)\chi_{N,0}(y)p_{N-n}(x)p_{N-m}(y)\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}w_{N}(y)^{1/2}dxdy=O(N^{-2}).

We also observe that
\text{supp}(\chi_{N,i}-\hat{\chi}_{N,i})\subseteq(\lambda_{i}-\delta/2,\lambda_{i}-\delta/32)\cup(\lambda_{i}+\delta/32,\lambda_{i}+\delta/2)
, and that
\chi_{N,i}-\hat{\chi}_{N,i}
satisfies the bounds (
7.1
). From this, we see that the proof of Lemma
7.2
with
\chi_{N,0}
replaced by
\chi_{N,i}-\hat{\chi}_{N,i}
establishes that

(7.48)

\frac{1}{2}\int\int\phi(x)\varphi(y)(\chi_{N,i}(x)-\hat{\chi}_{N,i}(x))\chi_{N,0}(y)p_{N-n}(x)p_{N-m}(y)\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}w_{N}(y)^{1/2}dxdy=

(7.49)

\frac{1}{2\pi N}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\phi(x)\varphi(x)(\chi_{N,i}(x)-\hat{\chi}_{N,i}(x))\chi_{N,0}(x)}{(1-x^{2})}\sin((m-n)\mathrm{arccos}(x))dx+O(N^{\gamma-2})=

(7.50)

\frac{1}{2\pi N}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\phi(x)\varphi(x)\chi_{N,i}(x)\chi_{N,0}(x)}{(1-x^{2})}\sin((m-n)\mathrm{arccos}(x))dx+O(N^{\gamma-2})

where in the last step we have used that
\hat{\chi}_{N,i}\chi_{N,0}(x)=0
. Combining (
7.47
) with (
7.50
) establishes (
7.36
) in this case. The case of
i=0
and
j>0
of (
7.36
) follows symmetrically, so we now assume that
i,j>0
.

We note that if additionally
i\neq j
, then (
7.36
) follows from (
7.34
) by employing the disjointness of the support of
\chi_{N,i}
and
\chi_{N,j}
(
6.2
) to rewrite this as a product of single integrals. Thus we assume that
i=j
and write, as before,
\lambda_{i}=\lambda
. As before, we additionally note that by the proof of (
7.34
), we have that

(7.51)

\int\int\phi(x)\varphi(y)\chi_{N,i}(x)\chi_{N,i}(y)p_{N-n}(x)p_{N-m}(y)\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}w_{N}(y)^{1/2}dxdy=

(7.52)

\int_{\lambda}^{\infty}\int_{\lambda}^{\infty}\phi(x)\varphi(y)\chi_{N,i}(x)\chi_{N,i}(y)p_{N-n}(x)p_{N-m}(y)\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}w_{N}(y)^{1/2}dxdy+

(7.53)

\int_{-\infty}^{\lambda}\int_{-\infty}^{\lambda}\phi(x)\varphi(y)\chi_{N,i}(x)\chi_{N,i}(y)p_{N-n}(x)p_{N-m}(y)\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}w_{N}(y)^{1/2}dxdy+O(N^{\gamma-2}).

We will compute the asymptotics of the first integral on the right-hand side, the other being identical. Proceeding as in Lemma
6.6
we may rewrite this integral as the sum
I+II+III
with

(7.54)

I=\frac{1}{\pi}\sum_{k,l=1,2}\int_{\lambda}^{\infty}[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(g_{N,n}(x))]_{k}(\int_{\lambda}^{g_{N,m}(x)}[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(z)]_{l}dz)\frac{h_{N,k}(x)l_{N,l}(x)\chi_{N,i}(x)^{2}}{g_{N,m}^{\prime}(x)}dx,

(7.55)

II=\frac{1}{\pi}\sum_{k,l=1,2}\int_{\lambda}^{\infty}(\int_{\lambda}^{g_{N,n}(x)}[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(z)]_{k}dz)(\int_{\lambda}^{g_{N,m}(x)}[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(z)]_{l}dz)\frac{\chi_{N,i}(x)h_{N,k}(x)}{g_{N,n}^{\prime}(x)}\frac{d}{dx}(\frac{\chi_{N,i}(x)l_{N,l}(x)}{g_{N,m}^{\prime}(x)})dx,

(7.56)

III=\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{k,l=1,2}\int_{\lambda}^{\infty}\int_{\lambda}^{\infty}(\int_{\lambda}^{g_{N,n}(x)}[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(z)]_{k}dz)(\int_{\lambda}^{g_{N,m}(y)}[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(z)]_{l}dz)\times

(7.57)

\frac{d}{dx}(\frac{\chi_{N,i}(x)h_{N,k}(x)}{g_{N,n}^{\prime}(x)})\frac{d}{dy}(\frac{\chi_{N,i}(y)l_{N,l}(y)}{g_{N,m}^{\prime}(y)})\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)dxdy,

where
l_{N,k},h_{N,k}
are as in the proof of Lemma
6.6
. We note that for any
c\in\mathbb{N}
, we have that
l_{N,k}^{(c)}(x),h_{N,k}^{(c)}(x)=O(1)
, which when supplemented with the asymptotics of (
6.68
) establish that there is
C
such that

(7.58)

|III|\leq\frac{C}{N^{2}}\int\int(|\chi_{N,i}^{\prime}(x)|+|\chi_{N,i}(x)|)(|\chi_{N,i}(y)|+|\chi_{N,i}^{\prime}(y)|)dxdy,

which shows that
III=O(N^{-2})
. Similarly

(7.59)

|II|\leq\frac{C}{N^{2}}(\sup_{x}|\chi_{N,i}(x)|)(\int|\chi_{N,i}^{\prime}(x)|dx+\int|\chi_{N,i}(x)|dx),

so that
II=O(N^{-2})
. As before, we may write
I
as the sum of two terms

(7.60)

I^{\prime}=-\sum_{k,l=1,2}\int_{\lambda}^{\infty}\sqrt{g_{N,n}(x)}J_{\alpha-(-1)^{k}1/2}(g_{N,n}(x))I_{\alpha-(-1)^{l}1/2,1/2}(g_{N,m}(x))\frac{h_{N,k}(x)l_{N,l}(x)\chi_{N,i}(x)^{2}}{g_{N,m}^{\prime}(x)}dx

(7.61)

II^{\prime}=\sum_{k,l=1,2}D(\alpha-(-1)^{l}1/2,1/2)\int_{\lambda}^{\infty}J_{\alpha-(-1)^{k}1/2}(g_{N,n}(x))\frac{h_{N,k}(x)l_{N,l}(x)\chi_{N,i}(x)^{2}}{g_{N,m}^{\prime}(x)}dx.

Employing Lemma
6.7
, we see that there is
C
such that

(7.62)

|II^{\prime}|\leq\frac{C}{N^{2}}+\frac{C}{N^{3}}\sum_{l=0}^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}|\frac{d^{l}}{dx^{l}}(\chi_{N,i}(x)^{2})|dx\leq

(7.63)

C^{2}\bigg(N^{-2}+N^{-3}(\sup_{x}|\chi_{N,i}(x)|)\sum_{l=0}^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}|\chi_{N,i}^{(l)}(x)|dx+N^{-3}(\sup_{x}|\chi_{N,i}^{\prime}(x)|)\int_{0}^{\infty}|\chi_{N,i}^{\prime}(x)|dx\bigg)=C^{3}(N^{-2}+N^{\gamma-3}).

Employing the asymptotics (
6.67
) and (
4.71
), as in the proof of Lemma
6.6
, we see that

(7.64)

I^{\prime}=\frac{1}{(N-m)\pi}\sum_{k,l=1,2}\int_{\lambda}^{\infty}[\mathcal{I}_{\alpha}(g_{N,n}(x))]_{k}[\mathcal{I}_{\alpha-1}(g_{N,m}(x))]_{l}\frac{h_{N,k}(x)l_{N,l}(x)\chi_{N,i}(x)^{2}}{f_{N,m,\lambda}^{\prime}(x)}dx+O(N^{-2}).

Now using the fact that
R_{N}(x),\bar{R}_{N}(x)=O(N^{\gamma-1})
for
x\in(\lambda-\delta_{N},\lambda+\delta_{N})
, we derive that

(7.65)

I^{\prime}=\frac{1}{(N-m)\pi}\sum_{k,l=1,2}\int_{\lambda}^{\infty}[\mathcal{I}_{\alpha}(g_{N,n}(x))]_{k}[\mathcal{I}_{\alpha-1}(g_{N,m}(x))]_{l}\phi(x)\varphi(x)\chi_{N,i}(x)^{2}\times

(7.66)

\frac{[T_{N,n,\lambda}(x)]_{1k}[T_{N,m,\lambda}(x)]_{1l}}{f_{N,m,\lambda}^{\prime}(x)}dx+O(N^{\gamma-2}).

The remainder of the proof of (
7.36
) now proceeds identically to that of Lemma
6.6
.

To show (
7.38
) one proceeds identically to the case of (
7.36
) above, with the only differences being that now the error in (
7.53
) is given by
O(N^{-1-\min(1,2(1-\gamma))})
,
II,III=O(N^{\gamma-2})
and
II^{\prime}=O(N^{-\min(3-2\gamma,2)})
. The
case of (
7.39
) is similar as well, with the error in (
7.50
) now being given by
O(N^{2(\gamma-1)})
, the error in (
7.53
) now being given by
O(N^{-2\min(1,2(1-\gamma))})
, and with the new error bounds
II,III=O(N^{2(\gamma-1)})
and
II^{\prime}=O(N^{-\min(3(1-\gamma),2)})

∎

Now we will now discuss the remaining asymptotics which we need outside of
(\lambda_{2}-\delta,\lambda_{1}+\delta)
. As we will not need
N
-dependant partitions in this region, and as the bound on the error term given in Proposition
4.16
is still of order
O(N^{-1})
, we see that the proofs given for Lemmas
6.1
,
6.2
, and
6.10
, require no modifications on their respective regions. Explicitly, together they imply the following result.

Lemma 7.4
.

Let
\phi
and
\varphi
be smooth functions of subexponential growth, both vanishing on
(\lambda_{2}-\delta,\lambda_{1}+\delta)
. Then we have that for any fixed
n
and
m
that

(7.67)

\int\phi(x)p_{N-k}(x)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}dx=O(N^{-1/2}).

(7.68)

\int J_{N}^{-1}(\varphi p_{N-m})(x)\phi(x)p_{N-n}(x)w_{N}(x)dx=\frac{1}{2\pi N}[\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\phi(x)\varphi(x)}{(1-x^{2})}\sin((m-n)\mathrm{arccos}(x))dx]+

(7.69)

\frac{1}{4N}[(-1)^{N-m}\phi(1)\varphi(-1)-(-1)^{N-n}\phi(-1)\varphi(1)]+O(N^{-7/6}).

Combining this Lemma with the above results, we achieve the following.

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
\phi
and
\varphi
be smooth functions of subexponential growth. Then we have that for any fixed
n
and
m
that

(7.70)

\int J_{N}^{-1}(\varphi p_{N-m})(x)\phi(x)p_{N-n}(x)w_{N}(x)dx=\frac{1}{2\pi N}[\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\phi(x)\varphi(x)}{(1-x^{2})}\sin((m-n)\mathrm{arccos}(x))dx]+

(7.71)

\frac{1}{4N}[(-1)^{N-m}\phi(1)\varphi(-1)-(-1)^{N-n}\phi(-1)\varphi(1)]+O(N^{-\min(2-\gamma,7/6)}).

Proceeding as in the prior section, we see that this result implies that when
N
is even that

(7.72)

\int J_{N}^{-1}(p_{N})(x)p_{N-1}(x)w_{N}(x)dx=O(N^{-\min(2-\gamma,7/6)}),

which is the first result required by Proposition
2.13
.

We will now begin discussing the required integrals involving
\ell_{i}
and
q_{i}
. A technicality present in this case is that the term
c_{i}
defined in Proposition
4.14
is now
N
-dependant and diverging as
|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|\to 0
. To avoid confusion, we will use the notation
c_{i,N}
for the remainder of the section to emphasize this
N
-dependence. We also observe that for
i\in\{1,2\}
, both
c_{i,N}|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|^{\alpha}
and
c_{i,N}^{-1}|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|^{-\alpha}
are uniformly bounded for
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\in(-1+\epsilon,1-\epsilon)
.

Lemma 7.6
.

For
\phi
a smooth function supported on
(-1+\delta,1-\delta)
, and any choice of
i\in{1,2}
and
j\in\{1,2,3\}
, we have that

(7.73)

\int\phi(x)\chi_{N,j}(x)\ell_{i}(x)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}dx=c_{i,N}^{-1}O(N^{\gamma-1}),\;\;\int\phi(x)\chi_{N,j}(x)q_{i}(x)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}dx=c_{i,N}O(1).

Proof.

In sight of Proposition
4.17
, and proceeding as in the proof of Lemma
6.13
, we are reduced to showing that

(7.74)

\int\phi(x)\chi_{N,j,i}(x)\begin{bmatrix}\pi^{1/2}p_{N}(x)\\
-i\pi^{1/2}p_{N-1}(x)\end{bmatrix}w_{N}(x)^{1/2}dx=T_{N,\lambda_{i}}(\lambda_{i})\begin{bmatrix}O(1)\\
0\end{bmatrix}+O(N^{\gamma-1}).

We will actually show that the error is of order
O(N^{-1})
, but as the error terms in Proposition
4.17
are of order
O(N^{\gamma-1})
, this does not improve the result. Now in the case that
i\neq j
, (
7.74
) follows from (
7.37
), and thus we will focus on the case of
i=j
, and denote
\lambda=\lambda_{i}
and
\alpha=\alpha_{i}
as before. Applying Proposition
4.17
we may write

(7.75)

\int\phi(x)\chi_{N,i,i}(x)\begin{bmatrix}\pi^{1/2}p_{N}(x)\\
-i\pi^{1/2}p_{N-1}(x)\end{bmatrix}w_{N}(x)^{1/2}dx=\int\frac{\xi_{N}(x)G_{N,1}(x)}{x}\mathrm{Sign}(x)\sqrt{\pi|Nx|}J_{\alpha+1/2}(N|x|)dx+

(7.76)

\int\frac{\xi_{N}(x)G_{N,2}(x)}{x}\sqrt{\pi|Nx|}J_{\alpha-1/2}(N|x|)dx=I+II,

where
G_{N,i}
is defined as in the proof of Lemma
6.13
, and
\xi_{N}(x)=\chi_{N,i,i}(f_{\lambda}^{-1}(x))x
. By Lemma
6.7
we have that

(7.77)

I=N^{-1}2\pi^{1/2}D(\alpha+1/2,-1/2)G_{N,1}(0)\xi_{N}(0)+E_{N},

where there is
C
such that

(7.78)

|E_{N}|\leq\frac{C}{N^{2}}\sum_{l=0}^{2}\int|\chi_{N,i}^{(l)}(x)|dx\leq C^{2}N^{\gamma-2}.

Thus
I=O(N^{-1})
. Proceeding similarly, one obtains that
II=O(N^{-1})
.
∎

As in (
6.181
), we see from the asymptotics provided in Proposition
4.17
that for
i\in\{1,2\}
, there are constants
C_{k,N}
, for
k\in\{1,2,3,4\}
, such that

(7.79)

c_{i,N}\ell_{i}(x)=C_{1,N}\frac{p_{N}(x)}{x-\lambda_{i}}+C_{2,N}\frac{p_{N-1}(x)}{x-\lambda_{i}},

(7.80)

c_{i,N}^{-1}q_{i}(x)=C_{3,N}\frac{p_{N}(x)}{x-\lambda_{i}}+C_{4,N}\frac{p_{N-1}(x)}{x-\lambda_{i}},

and such that
C_{k,N}=O(1)
for
k\in\{1,2,3,4\}
.

We recall the functions
\hat{\chi}_{N,i}
introduced in the proof of Lemma
7.3
, which are similar to
\chi_{N,i}
, but defined so that
\text{supp}(\hat{\chi}_{N,i})\cap\text{supp}(\chi_{N,0})=\varnothing
. Proceeding as in the previous section, we see that

(7.81)

\int J_{N}^{-1}\ell_{l}(x)p_{N-1}(x)w_{N}(x)dx=\int(J_{N}^{-1}\chi_{N,l}\ell_{l})(x)\hat{\chi}_{N,l}(x)p_{N-1}(x)w_{N}(x)dx+c_{l,N}^{-1}O(N^{-\min(1,(1-\gamma)-1/2)}),

(7.82)

\int J_{N}^{-1}p_{N}(x)q_{k}(x)w_{N}(x)dx=\int(J_{N}^{-1}\chi_{N,l}p_{N})(x)\hat{\chi}_{N,k}(x)q_{k}(x)w_{N}(x)dx+c_{k,N}O(N^{-1/2}),

(7.83)

\int J_{N}^{-1}\ell_{l}(x)q_{k}(x)w_{N}(x)dx=\delta_{k,l}\int J_{N}^{-1}(\chi_{N,l}\ell_{l})(x)\hat{\chi}_{N,k}(x)q_{k}(x)w_{N}(x)dx+c_{k,N}c^{-1}_{l,N}O(N^{-\min(1/2,2(1-\gamma))}).

We observe that
c_{k,N}c^{-1}_{l,N}=O(1)
. By these formulas, and (
7.79
-
7.80
), we see that to establish the remaining cases of Proposition
2.13
, it suffices to show the following.

Lemma 7.7
.

Let
\phi
and
\varphi
be smooth. Then for any choice of
i=1,2
, and any
n
and
m
, we have that

(7.84)

\int\int\phi(x)\varphi(y)\chi_{N,i,i}(x)\chi_{N,i}(y)p_{N-n}(x)p_{N-m}(y)\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}w_{N}(y)^{1/2}dxdy=O(N^{-1}\log(N)).

Moreover, if
|n-m|\leq 1
, then additionally

(7.85)

\int\int\phi(x)\varphi(y)\chi_{N,i,i}(x)\chi_{N,i,i}(y)p_{N-n}(x)p_{N-m}(y)\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)w_{N}(x)^{1/2}w_{N}(y)^{1/2}dxdy=O(N^{\gamma-1}\log(N)).

Proof.

We begin with the proof of (
7.84
). We may proceed identically to the proof of (
6.191
) to write the left-hand side of (
7.84
) as the sum
I+II
where

(7.86)

I=\frac{1}{\pi}\sum_{k,l=1,2}\int[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(g_{N,m}(x))]_{l}I_{k}(g_{N,n}(x))\frac{h_{N,k}(x)l_{N,l}(x)g_{N,n}(x)\chi_{N,i}(x)^{2}}{g_{N,n}^{\prime}(x)(x-\lambda)}dx,

(7.87)

II=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{k,l=1,2}\int\int[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(g_{N,m}(x))]_{l}I_{k}(g_{N,n}(y))\chi_{N,i}(y)l_{N,l}(y)\frac{d}{dx}(\frac{\chi_{N,i}(x)h_{N,k}(x)g_{N,n}(x)}{g_{N,n}^{\prime}(x)(x-\lambda)})\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)dxdy,

with notation as in the proof of Lemma
6.14
. As
|\chi_{N,j}(x)|
is bounded, and as the integrand of
I
remains of order
O(\min(N|x|^{-1},1))
, we see that
I=O(N^{-1}\log(N))
, as before. In addition, we see there is
C
such that

(7.88)

|II|\leq\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{k,l=1,2}\int|\chi_{N,i}(y)I_{k}(g_{N,n}(y))l_{N,l}(y)|dy\int|[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(g_{N,m}(x))]_{l}\frac{d}{dx}(\frac{\chi_{N,i}(x)h_{N,k}(x)g_{N,n}(x)}{g_{N,n}^{\prime}(x)(x-\lambda)})|dx\leq

(7.89)

\frac{C}{N}\int\min(|y|^{-1},N)dy\int(|\chi_{N,i}(x)|+|\chi_{N,i}^{\prime}(x)|)dx=O(N^{-1}\log(N)).

This completes the proof of (
7.84
).

We now discuss the proof of (
7.85
). The proof of (
7.85
) may also be reduced, by modifying the proof of (
6.192
) as above, to showing that for
k,l=1,2
,

(7.90)

\int\int\frac{\chi_{N,i}(x-\lambda)\chi_{N,i}(y-\lambda)}{xy}[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(Nx)]_{k}[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(Ny)]_{l}\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)dxdy=O(N^{\gamma-1}\log(N)).

On the other hand, we may rescale the left-hand side of (
7.90
) to become

(7.91)

\int\int\frac{\chi(x)\chi(y)}{xy}[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(N\delta_{N}x)]_{k}[\mathcal{J}_{\alpha}(N\delta_{N}y)]_{l}\mathrm{Sign}(x-y)dxdy.

This coincides with a rescaled case of (
6.201
), which we have already shown to be of order

(7.92)

O(\delta_{N}^{-1}N^{-1}\log(\delta_{N}^{-1}N^{-1})),

and thus is further of order
O(N^{\gamma-1}\log(N))
. This completes the proof of (
7.90
).
∎
